Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me, I really appreciate it. Um, I hope I'm not wasting your time with this, but I'm here because... well, for a long time now, my sleep has just been kind of broken.

Well, um... it's been quite a while now. I'd say... a little over two years? 

It kind of started right around the time my mother-in-law moved in with us. I mean, please don't get me wrong, I love her dearly and I'm so glad we can be there to help her. Well, um... I really try to focus on the positive days, you know? I don't want to sound like I'm just miserable all the time, because I'm really not.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, like I was mentioning just a little bit ago, it's... it's been pretty much there this whole time. 

I really wish I could tell you there were a few good months in there where everything felt easy and light again. I try so hard to keep my energy up, especially for work, because my patients really need me to be present for them. I force myself to stay motivated to get my tasks done, so I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing. 

But that heavy feeling... where it just takes so much extra effort to do normal things... I don't think it's really let up for any real stretch of time. It's just sort of this constant background noise. I'm sorry, I know I'm probably just repeating myself now. I hope I'm not sounding too difficult or like a broken record. I'm trying to explain it as best as I can, is this helpful at all?

No, nothing like that. \n\nI mean, I know things have been hard, and I've been feeling so down, but... I would never do something like that. I have my husband, and my mother-in-law to take care of, and my patients rely on me so much. I could never just... leave them like that. It would be so terribly selfish of me, wouldn't it? \n\nI just want to get back to being myself so I can be a better help to everyone. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I still try very hard to participate in things, you know? I still go to Sunday dinner with the family, and I try to look at the art I've always liked... I don't want anyone to think I'm withdrawing or being difficult. I really do make an effort to be present and cheerful for them.

But... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't sound ungrateful because I know I have a good life... most days, it just feels like the color has gone out of things. Even when I'm sitting right there at the table with everyone, or looking at something beautiful... I just feel this... this sadness. And I have to work so, so hard not to just tear up for no clear reason. I don't want to ruin the mood for anyone else, so I try to hide it. 

I'm sorry, I know that sounds terrible. I should be enjoying those things, shouldn't I? I really hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself. I'm trying to be better, I really am. Is it okay that I'm feeling this way?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really try not to let it interfere, you know? I still show up for all my shifts, and I make sure my clients are safe and cared for. I don't want you to think I'm doing a bad job or letting anyone down. 

But... I guess it's more than just being in the background. It's... well, I've noticed I'm starting to fall behind on my client documentation. I always get it done eventually, I promise! It just takes me so much longer now. And... I used to volunteer to mentor the newer aides, but I just haven't been doing that lately. 

It's just that keeping up with the basics at work, and then making sure things are okay at home... it just takes everything I have right now. There's nothing left over. I feel terrible about the paperwork and not helping the new girls, I really do. I'm trying to get back on top of it. Does that make sense? I hope that answers your question okay.

Like I was just saying, it's that feeling that the color has just completely gone out of everything. And the crying... it happens right in the middle of Sunday dinner, or when I'm looking at art I usually love. I just have to work so, so hard to swallow it down and not tear up, especially since there's really no clear reason for it. \n\nI really don't want to make anyone else sad or bring down the room, you know? So I just try to keep smiling through it. Is... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... it's been quite a while now. I'd say... a little over two years? \n\nIt kind of started right around the time my mother-in-law moved in with us. I mean, please don't get me wrong, I love her dearly and I'm so glad we can be there to help her. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining about having her in our home! Family is so important, and I know it's a blessing to have her with us. \n\nBut... yes, that just seems to be when this all began, a little over two years ago. I hope it's okay that I brought that up... I don't want to sound selfish or like I'm blaming her for anything. Does that give you the timeline you needed?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling better in the past two years, or has it been a constant feeling of sadness?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to focus on the positive days, you know? I don't want to sound like I'm just miserable all the time, because I'm really not. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest... I don't think there's been a time where it completely went away? I mean, there are definitely days where it's easier to push through and get things done, and I always make sure to keep up with my patients and everything at home. I'd hate for anyone to think I'm letting my responsibilities slide. \n\nBut that underlying feeling, that sort of grayness... I don't think it's ever fully lifted in these past couple of years. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds terribly pessimistic. I really do try to look on the bright side. Is that... does that answer your question properly?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicide in the past two years?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. No, nothing like that. \n\nI mean, I know things have been hard, and I've been feeling so down, but... I would never do something like that. I have my husband, and my mother-in-law to take care of, and my patients rely on me so much. I could never just... leave them like that. It would be so terribly selfish of me, wouldn't it? \n\nI just want to get back to being myself so I can be a better help to everyone. I really hope you don't think I'm the sort of person who would just give up. I'm trying very hard, I promise. Is... is it normal that you have to ask that? I hope I didn't say something earlier that made you worry I might do something awful.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess I just haven't been quite as hungry lately. It's nothing serious, really! I just... I don't have much of an appetite anymore. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really don't want to complain, because family is so important, you know? And I love my mother-in-law, I really do. We're happy to have her. But... if I think about when this really started feeling like this... it's been a little over two years now. Right around the time she moved in with us. 

I feel so guilty even saying that out loud. It's not her fault at all, she's a lovely woman and she needs our help. I just... I think the adjustment was harder on me than I expected, and then this feeling just sort of settled in and never left. It hasn't really gotten worse, it's just... been there ever since. 

I hope that doesn't make me sound like a terrible daughter-in-law. I really do my best to make sure she's comfortable and happy here. Is it normal that a change like that would trigger something like this for so long?
